Viscous approximation of triangular system in 1-d with nonlinear viscosity
Boris Haspot, Animesh Jana

Abstract
We study the vanishing viscosity limit for 2 × 2 \displaystyle 2\times 2 triangular system of hyperbolic conservation laws when the viscosity coefficients are non linear. In this article, we assume that the viscosity matrix B ⁡ ( u ) \displaystyle B(u) is commutating with the convective part A ⁡ ( u ) \displaystyle A(u) . We show the existence of global smooth solution to the parabolic equation satisfying uniform total variation bound in ε \displaystyle\varepsilon provided that the initial data is small in B ​ V \displaystyle BV . This extends the previous result of Bianchini and Bressan [Commun. Pure Appl. Anal. (2002)] which was considering the case B ⁡ ( u ) = I \displaystyle B(u)=I .



1
Introduction

It is well known that hyperbolic systems generally generate infinitely many weak solutions, in order to overcome this lack of uniqueness it is relevant to consider processes of vanishing viscous limits in order to select the relevant physical solutions for systems of nonlinear hyperbolic systems.
In this article, we are interested in studying the vanishing viscosity limit for the following hyperbolic triangular system in one dimension which reads as follows

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}u_{1,t}+(f(u_{1}))_{x}&=0,\\
u_{2,t}+(g(u_{1},u_{2}))_{x}&=0,\end{array}\mbox{ for }x\in\mathbb{R}\mbox{ and }t>0,\right.

(1.1)

where
\displaystyle f
and
\displaystyle g
are smooth functions. This system appears in various physics and engineering models, including multi-component chromatography, two-phase flow in porous media and sedimentation processes (see
[
5
,
15
,
16
]
and references therein for more detailed discussion on applications of the system). Here, we consider the flux functions
\displaystyle f
and
\displaystyle g
such that the system of equations (
1.1
) becomes strictly hyperbolic (see (
1.6
) below for more precise condition). We study now the following viscosity approximation,

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} u_{1,t}+(f(u_{1}))_{x}&=\varepsilon(\alpha_{1}(u_{1})u_{1,x})_{x},\\
u_{2,t}+(g(u_{1},u_{2}))_{x}&=\varepsilon\left[(\beta(u_{1},u_{2})u_{1,x})_{x}+(\alpha_{2}(u_{1},u_{2})u_{2,x})_{x}\right],\end{aligned}\end{cases}

(1.2)

with
\displaystyle\varepsilon>0
. The system (
1.2
) is supplemented with the following initial condition,

u(0,\cdot)=\bar{u},

(1.3)

where
\displaystyle\bar{u}
will be assumed small in
\displaystyle TV
, the Total Variation.
In this article, we prove the global existence of smooth solution to (
1.2
) and study the vanishing viscosity limit, that is, to show that the limit of
\displaystyle(u_{1}^{\varepsilon},u_{2}^{\varepsilon})
as
\displaystyle\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
is solution of the system (
1.1
).
We can write (
1.1
) in the following form

u_{t}+A(u)u_{x}=0,

(1.4)

where
\displaystyle A(u)
is determined as follows

A(u)=\begin{pmatrix}f^{\prime}(u_{1})&0\\
\frac{\partial g}{\partial u_{1}}&\frac{\partial g}{\partial u_{2}}\end{pmatrix}\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;u=\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}\\
u_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

(1.5)

We can note that the eigenvalues of
\displaystyle A(u)
are
\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(u)=f^{\prime}(u_{1})
and
\displaystyle\lambda_{2}(u)=\frac{\partial g}{\partial u_{2}}(u)
. We assume now the following strict hyperbolicity condition:

\lambda_{2}(u)-\lambda_{1}(u)=\frac{\partial g}{\partial u_{2}}(u)-f^{\prime}(u_{1})\geq c>0,

(1.6)

for
\displaystyle u
in a neighborhood of a compact
\displaystyle K
with
\displaystyle\{\bar{u}(x),x\in\mathbb{R}\}
included in
\displaystyle K
. It is well known that under the condition (
1.6
) the system (
1.1
) admits a unique global solution provided that the initial data
\displaystyle u(0)=\bar{u}
is sufficiently small in
\displaystyle BV(\mathbb{R})
(see
[
4
,
6
,
12
]
), the uniqueness is defined in a suitable class of solutions which satisfied in particular the
Liu condition
(we refer also to
[
7
]
). To study the vanishing viscosity limit for (
1.1
) we recall that we assume that
\displaystyle\bar{u}
is small in
\displaystyle BV(\mathbb{R})
so that we enter in the framework of the theory of existence and uniqueness previously mentionned. Next, we would like to write the parabolic system of equations (
1.2
) in the following form

u_{t}+A(u)u_{x}=\varepsilon(B(u)u_{x})_{x},

(1.7)

where
\displaystyle B
is defined as follows

B(u)=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}(u_{1})&0\\
\beta(u)&\alpha_{2}(u)\end{pmatrix}.

(1.8)

In addition, we assume that the coefficients
\displaystyle\alpha_{i}
are smooth functions such that there exists
\displaystyle c_{1},M>0
such that for any
\displaystyle u\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
we have for any
\displaystyle i\in\{1,2\}

0<c_{1}\leq\alpha_{i}(u)\leq M<+\infty.

(1.9)

In the sequel we deal with a viscosity matrix
\displaystyle B(u)
which commutate with the convective matrix
\displaystyle A(u)
. Note that to satisfy the condition
\displaystyle AB=BA
, the function
\displaystyle\beta(u)
must satisfy the following condition

\beta(u)=(\alpha_{1}(u_{1})-\alpha_{2}(u))\gamma(u)\mbox{ where }\gamma(u)=\frac{\frac{\partial g}{\partial u_{1}}(u)}{f^{\prime}(u_{1})-\frac{\partial g}{\partial u_{2}}(u)}.

(1.10)

This yields that the matrices
\displaystyle A(u),B(u)
have same eigenvectors for any
\displaystyle u\in K
.

We can observe that by using the following rescaling of coordinates
\displaystyle s=\frac{t}{\varepsilon}
and
\displaystyle y=\frac{x}{\varepsilon}
we are reduced to study the following system :

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &u_{s}+A(u)u_{y}=(B(u)u_{y})_{y},\\
&u(0,\cdot)=\bar{u}(\varepsilon\cdot).\end{aligned}\end{cases}

(1.11)

We point out here that the total variation of
\displaystyle\bar{u}^{\varepsilon}
is independent of
\displaystyle\varepsilon
, in other term the
\displaystyle BV
space is invariant by the scaling
\displaystyle x\rightarrow\varepsilon x
. We are finally reduced to study the system (
1.2
) with
\displaystyle\varepsilon=1
.

The study of vanishing viscosity limit for system of conservation laws can be found in
[
2
,
3
,
4
,
8
,
9
,
13
,
17
]
including in particular the so called case of compressible Euler equations when we consider the viscous approximation issue of the compressible Navier-Stokes system (see in particular
[
8
,
9
]
, it is important to point out that the study is particularly delicate in this case since the
the viscosity is only partial, in other words
\displaystyle B(u)
is not invertible). We refer to
[
1
,
18
]
for the study of vanishing viscosity limit for system of conservation laws with boundaries. Here, we briefly discuss a few of them which are relevant to the setting and techniques of the current article. When the convective matrix
\displaystyle A
belongs to the Temple class, that is when each characteristic is a straight line, the existence, the uniform
\displaystyle TV
estimates in
\displaystyle\varepsilon
and the
\displaystyle L^{1}
Lipschitz estimate for the system (
1.7
) are established in
[
2
]
for
\displaystyle B=I
. We have studied the vanishing viscosity limit for the Temple class hyperbolic system in
[
14
]
when the matrices
\displaystyle A
and
\displaystyle B
share same eigenvectors. Furthermore, Bianchini and Bressan
[
3
,
4
]
developed a powerful method for studying the vanishing viscosity limit by decomposing the gradient of solution as a sum of the gradient of viscous travelling wave and by introducing suitable functionals for carefully analyzing all
interaction terms. In other words Bianchini and Bressan decompose the solution
\displaystyle u_{x}=\sum_{i}v_{i}\tilde{r}_{i}(u,v_{i},\sigma_{i})
into scalar components in a basis
\displaystyle(\tilde{r}_{i}(u,v_{i},\sigma_{i}))_{\{1\leq i\leq n\}}
selected by a center manifold technique which is suitable inasmuch as it enables to estimates in particular the
\displaystyle TV
bound of the viscous travelling wave. It is important to point out that generally in the purely hyperbolic case without viscosity, we decompose
\displaystyle u_{x}
along a basis
\displaystyle\{r_{1}(u),\cdots,r_{n}(u)\}
of eigenvectors of the
matrix
\displaystyle A(u)
, however this choice does not work here due to the fact that gradient of the
\displaystyle i
-viscous travelling waves
\displaystyle U^{\prime}
is not necessary collinear to
\displaystyle r_{i}(U)
.
In particular this aspect is crucial for obtaining BV estimates when
\displaystyle A
is not in Temple class (in fact when
\displaystyle A
is Temple we can
a priori
decompose the gradient
\displaystyle u_{x}
in the basis
\displaystyle\{r_{1}(u),\cdots,r_{n}(u)\}
provided that
\displaystyle A
and
\displaystyle B
are commutating because in this case gradient of the
\displaystyle i
-viscous travelling wave are collinear to
\displaystyle r_{i}(U)
). In these articles
[
3
,
4
]
, the viscosity matrix
\displaystyle B
is taken to be
\displaystyle I
. Here we study the vanishing viscosity limit for triangular system (
1.1
) when
\displaystyle A
and
\displaystyle B
have same eigenvectors. Now, we would like to highlight the following key points of the current article.

•

It deals with a class of nonlinear viscosity matrix
\displaystyle B(u)
when the hyperbolic part
\displaystyle A(u)
is not Temple class. This extends the result of
[
3
]
from
\displaystyle B(u)=I
to the class of matrices which are diagonalizable with strictly positive eigenvalues and satisfying the relation
\displaystyle AB=BA
. Since
\displaystyle A(u)
is not necessarily Temple class the gradient of the viscous travelling waves are not in the direction of eigenvectors. Therefore, as in
[
3
,
4
]
we need to decompose
\displaystyle u_{x}
in the basis of gradient of travelling waves as follows
\displaystyle u_{x}=\sum_{i=1}^{2}v_{i}\tilde{r}_{i}(u,v_{i},\sigma_{i})
. In particular each
\displaystyle v_{i}
satisfies the following equation:

\partial_{t}v_{i}+\partial_{x}(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}v_{i})-\partial_{x}(\alpha_{i}\partial_{x}v_{i})=\phi_{i},

with
\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
well chosen and
\displaystyle\phi_{i}
a remainder term.
Due to the fact that we deal with a variable viscosity matrix
\displaystyle B(u)
, we get new terms in
\displaystyle\phi_{i}
the forcing part of the parabolic equations which govern
\displaystyle v_{i}
. As in
[
3
,
4
]
, the proof is decomposed in two part, the first one consists in collecting parabolic estimates on the solution
\displaystyle u
which allows in particular to prove that
\displaystyle\|u_{x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}
remains small until a time
\displaystyle\hat{t}
. This time
\displaystyle\hat{t}
can be seen as a threshold time which delimits the parabolic behavior of the system and the hyperbolic behavior of the equations. In our case since we deal with variable viscosity coefficients, compared with
[
3
]
, we need to develop new parabolic estimates. To do this by a suitable change of variable on
\displaystyle u_{x}
we manage to diagonalize the system in a such way that we can extend the analysis of parabolic estimates done in
[
3
,
4
]
.

•

There exist new terms of the form
\displaystyle(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2})(w_{1,xx}v_{1}-w_{1}v_{1,xx})
in
\displaystyle\varphi_{2}
which does not appear in the analysis of
[
3
]
(here
\displaystyle v_{1}=u_{1,x}
and
\displaystyle w_{1}=u_{1,t}
). It is needless to say that for
\displaystyle\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}
these terms do not appear. In particular for
\displaystyle B=\alpha(u)I
we do not need to deal with such terms. To resolve this issue, we introduce a new variable
\displaystyle z
. We discuss below where we mention how this new variable enable us to estimate this new term in
\displaystyle L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}
norm.

To prove the global existence of smooth solution and uniform total variation estimate we adapt the method developed by Bianchini and Bressan
[
2
,
3
,
4
]
. As we have mentioned earlier, we need to decompose
\displaystyle u_{x}
in the basis of travelling waves which enables us to diagonalize the system, and to deal only with scalar equations on
\displaystyle v_{1}
and
\displaystyle v_{2}
. In our case
\displaystyle\phi_{1}=0

and
\displaystyle\phi_{2}
is composed of terms of the following form which exists in the analysis of
[
2
,
3
,
4
]

v_{1}v_{2},\,v_{1}v_{2,x},\,v_{1,x}v_{2},\,(w_{1,x}v_{1}-v_{1,x}w_{1}),\,v_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{v_{1}}\right)_{x}^{2}\chi_{\left\{\left|\frac{w_{1}}{v_{1}}\right|\leq\delta_{1}\right\}}\,\mbox{ and }w_{1}^{2}\chi_{\left\{\left|\frac{w_{1}}{v_{1}}\right|\geq\delta_{1}\right\}}.

Additionally, the force
\displaystyle\phi_{2}
contains a term of the form

(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2})(w_{1,xx}v_{1}-w_{1}v_{1,xx}),

which requires to be estimated in
\displaystyle L^{1}_{t,x}
norm.
In order to handle this new difficulty we introduce a new variable
\displaystyle z_{1}=\alpha_{1}w_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}w_{1}
which can be seen as
an effective flux
for the parabolic equation of
\displaystyle w_{1}
. Thanks to the structure of the system the new variable
\displaystyle z_{1}
satisfies a parabolic equation

z_{1,t}+(\tilde{\lambda}_{1}z_{1})_{x}-(\alpha_{1}z_{1,x})_{x}=\phi,

where remainder term
\displaystyle\phi
can be bound by
\displaystyle z_{1,x}v_{1}-z_{1}v_{1,x}
and
\displaystyle w_{1,x}v_{1}-w_{1}v_{1,x}
. See (
5.7
) for detailed definition of
\displaystyle\phi
. By adapting the area functional formula from Bianchini and Bressan
[
3
,
4
]
we can prove the required
\displaystyle L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}
estimate for the following terms

z_{1,x}v_{1}-z_{1}v_{1,x}.

Then we can estimate
\displaystyle v_{1,xx}w_{1}-w_{1,xx}v_{1}
by
\displaystyle z_{1,x}v_{1}-z_{1}v_{1,x}
and
\displaystyle w_{1,x}v_{1}-w_{1}v_{1,x}
by applying a bootstrap arguments.
In section
7
, we study the stability of solution. We consider an initial data
\displaystyle\bar{u}^{\theta}:=\theta\bar{u}+(1-\theta)\bar{v}
and
\displaystyle u^{\theta}
is the corresponding to initial data
\displaystyle\bar{u}^{\theta}
. As in
[
2
,
3
,
4
]
,
\displaystyle h:=\frac{\partial u}{\partial\theta}
satisfies a linear equation (see (
7.4
)). We decompose
\displaystyle h
in the basis of travelling waves. Then the unknowns
\displaystyle h_{1},h_{2}
satisfies parabolic equations with force. In the equation for
\displaystyle h_{2}
, there appears new term like
\displaystyle(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2})(h_{1,xx}v_{1}-h_{1}v_{1,xx})
and
\displaystyle(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2})(h_{1,xx}w_{1}-h_{1}w_{1,xx})
. We resolve the issue by defining a new quantity
\displaystyle\hat{h}_{1}=\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1}
. Again, due to the structure of the system, we obtain that
\displaystyle\hat{h}_{1}
satisfies the following parabolic equation,

\hat{h}_{1,t}+(\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{h}_{1})_{x}-(\alpha_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x})_{x}=\hat{\phi}_{1},

where remainder term
\displaystyle\hat{\phi}_{1}
can be bound by
\displaystyle\hat{h}_{1,x}v_{1}-\hat{h}_{1}v_{1,x}
,
\displaystyle h_{1,x}v_{1}-h_{1}v_{1,x}
and
\displaystyle w_{1,x}h_{1}-w_{1}h_{1,x}
. Again by using the area functional formula from Bianchini and Bressan
[
3
,
4
]
we can prove the required
\displaystyle L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}
estimate.

Rest of the article is organized as follows. In the next subsection
1.1
, we state the main result on existence and stability of global smooth solution for (
1.11
). In section
2
and
3
, we study the parabolic estimates of the solution and the decomposition of
\displaystyle u_{x}
respectively. The remainder terms are calculated in section
4
. We study the global existence of smooth solution by proving uniform total variation estimate which is followed from an interaction estimates which can be found in section
5
and
6
. We prove the stability in section
7
. Finally, we establish the vanishing viscosity limit in section
8
.

1.1
Presentation of the results

Now, we state the main result of the article.

Theorem 1.1
.

Consider the following hyperbolic system with viscosity,

u_{t}+A(u)u_{x}=(B(u)u_{x})_{x},\quad u(0,x)=\bar{u}(x),

(1.12)

where
\displaystyle A
and
\displaystyle B
are defined as in (
1.5
) and (
1.8
).
We assume that
\displaystyle f
and
\displaystyle g
are smooth functions verifying the strict hyperbolicity condition (
1.6
) in a neighborhood of a compact set
\displaystyle K\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}

and that
\displaystyle A
and
\displaystyle B
are commutating such that the condition (
1.10
) is satisfied.
There exists
\displaystyle L_{1},L_{2},L_{3}>0
and
\displaystyle\delta_{0}>0
such that the following holds. If
\displaystyle\bar{u}
satisfies

TV(\bar{u})\leq\delta_{0}\mbox{ and }\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}\bar{u}(x)=u^{*}\in K,

(1.13)

then there exists unique solution
\displaystyle u
to the Cauchy problem (
1.12
) and it satisfies the following properties for any
\displaystyle t,s\geq 0

\displaystyle\displaystyle TV(u(t))

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq L_{1}TV(\bar{u}),

(1.14)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u(t)-v(t)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq L_{2}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\bar{u}-\bar{v}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}},

(1.15)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u(t)-u(s)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq L_{3}\left(\left|t-s\right|+\left|\sqrt{t}-\sqrt{s}\right|\right),

(1.16)

where
\displaystyle v
is the unique solution of (
1.12
) with initial data
\displaystyle\bar{v}
satisfying (
1.13
).

Remark 1.2
.

We would like to point out that in the previous Theorem our analysis does not require to consider a conservative system. Indeed we could deal with a triangular matrix
\displaystyle A(u)
which does not satisfy necessary
\displaystyle A(u)=Df(u)
with
\displaystyle f=(f_{1},g)
.

We deduce the following results on the vanishing viscosity limit process.

Corollary 1.3
.

Consider the Cauchy problem hyperbolic system with vanishing viscosity,

u^{\varepsilon}_{t}+A(u^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon}_{x}=\varepsilon(B(u^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon}_{x})_{x},\quad u^{\varepsilon}(0,x)=\bar{u}(x).

(1.17)

We assume that the drift
\displaystyle A
and viscosity matrix
\displaystyle B
satisfy the same conditions as in the Theorem
1.1
. There exists
\displaystyle L_{1},L_{2},L_{3}>0
and
\displaystyle\delta_{0}>0
such that the following holds. If
\displaystyle\bar{u}
satisfies

TV(\bar{u})\leq\delta_{0}\mbox{ and }\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}\bar{u}(x)\in K,

(1.18)

for some compact set
\displaystyle K\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
then there exists unique solution
\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}
to the Cauchy problem (
1.17
) and it satisfies the following properties

\displaystyle\displaystyle TV(u^{\varepsilon}(t))

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq L_{1}TV(\bar{u}),

(1.19)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u^{\varepsilon}(t)-v^{\varepsilon}(t)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq L_{2}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\bar{u}-\bar{v}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}},

(1.20)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u^{\varepsilon}(t)-u^{\varepsilon}(s)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq L_{3}\left(\left|t-s\right|+\sqrt{\varepsilon}\left|\sqrt{t}-\sqrt{s}\right|\right),

(1.21)

where
\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}
is the unique solution corresponding to
\displaystyle\bar{v}
satisfying (
1.18
).

Furthermore, for any
\displaystyle t\geq 0
the sequence
\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon}(t))_{\varepsilon>0}
converges to
\displaystyle u(t)
in
\displaystyle L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R})
topology which is the unique solution to hyperbolic system (
1.1
).

2
Parabolic estimates

We wish now to prove different regularity estimates for solutions of (
1.11
). In the sequel we consider a global
\displaystyle BV
solution
\displaystyle u=u(t,x)
of (
1.11
) satisfying
\displaystyle\lim u(t,x)_{x\rightarrow-\infty}=u^{*}
, we observe easily that
\displaystyle u^{*}
does not depend on the time
\displaystyle t
. We denote by

\displaystyle P(u)=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-\gamma(u)&1\end{pmatrix}\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;P^{-1}(u)=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
\gamma(u)&1\end{pmatrix},

which satisfy

P(u)A(u)P^{-1}(u)=\begin{pmatrix}f^{\prime}_{1}(u_{1})&0\\
0&\frac{\partial g}{\partial u_{2}}(u)\end{pmatrix}\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;P(u)B(u)P^{-1}(u)=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}(u_{1})&0\\
0&\alpha_{2}(u)\end{pmatrix}.

(2.1)

Furthermore, we indicate the eigenvectors of
\displaystyle A(u)
as follows

r_{1}(u)=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
\gamma(u)\end{pmatrix}\mbox{and}\;r_{2}(u)=r_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}\mbox{ where }\gamma(u)=\frac{\frac{\partial g}{\partial u_{1}}(u)}{f^{\prime}(u_{1})-\frac{\partial g}{\partial u_{2}}(u)}.

(2.2)

We can note that the previous formula is well defined due to the strict hyperbolicity assumption (
1.6
).
We consider in the sequel the vectors
\displaystyle l_{i}
for
\displaystyle i\in\{1,2\}
with
\displaystyle(l_{i})_{j}=1
if
\displaystyle j=i
and
\displaystyle 0
otherwise.

Note that
\displaystyle A(u)=DF(u)
with
\displaystyle F=(f,g)^{T}
, it implies in particular that the system is conservative. In the sequel we consider
\displaystyle A_{1},B_{1}
defined as follows

A_{1}(u)=\begin{pmatrix}f^{\prime}_{1}(u_{1})&0\\
0&\frac{\partial g}{\partial u_{2}}(u)\end{pmatrix}\mbox{ and }B_{1}(u)=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}(u_{1})&0\\
0&\alpha_{2}(u)\end{pmatrix}.

Taking now the derivative of (
1.11
), we get:

\displaystyle u_{x,t}+A(u)u_{xx}=(B(u)u_{xx})_{x}+{\cal R},

(2.3)

with:

{\cal R}=\begin{pmatrix}(\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(u_{1})(u_{1,x})^{2})_{x}-f^{\prime\prime}(u_{1})(u_{1,x})^{2}\\
(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\beta(u))u_{1,x})_{x}+(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\alpha_{2}(u))u_{2,x})_{x}\end{pmatrix}

(2.4)

We wish now as in
[
14
]
to diagonalize the previous system, then we consider in this section the unknown
\displaystyle w=\begin{pmatrix}w_{1}\\
w_{2}\end{pmatrix}=P(u)u_{x}=\begin{pmatrix}u_{1,x}\\
-\gamma(u)u_{1,x}+u_{2,x}\end{pmatrix}
. We want to use the equation (
2.3
) and the matrix
\displaystyle P(u)
. The direct computations give

\displaystyle P(u)u_{x,t}=w_{t}-\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(\gamma(u))w_{1}\end{pmatrix},\;\;P(u)A(u)u_{xx}=A_{1}(u)w_{x}+A_{1}(u)\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\gamma(u))w_{1}\end{pmatrix}

(2.5)

\displaystyle P(u)(B(u)u_{xx})_{x}=P(u)B(u)u_{xxx}+P(u)\begin{pmatrix}\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(u_{1})u_{1,x}u_{1,xx}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\beta(u))u_{1,xx}+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\alpha_{2}(u))u_{2,xx}\end{pmatrix}.

Using the fact that
\displaystyle P(u)u_{xxx}=w_{xx}+{\cal R}_{1}
with
\displaystyle{\cal R}_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
2\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xx}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial^{2}x}(\gamma(u))u_{1,x}\end{pmatrix}
it follows that

\displaystyle P(u)B(u)u_{xxx}=B_{1}(u)w_{xx}+B_{1}(u){\cal R}_{1}

(2.6)

Setting

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\cal R}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(u_{1})u_{1,x}u_{1,xx}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\beta(u))u_{1,xx}+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\alpha_{2}(u))u_{2,xx}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\cal R}_{3}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(\gamma(u))u_{1,x}\end{pmatrix},{\cal R}_{4}=A_{1}(u)\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\gamma(u))u_{1,x}\end{pmatrix},

and combining (
2.3
), (
2.5
) and (
2.6
) it yields

w_{t}+A_{1}(u)w_{x}-B_{1}(u)w_{xx}=B_{1}(u){\cal R}_{1}+P(u){\cal R}_{2}+{\cal R}_{3}-{\cal R}_{4}+P(u){\cal R}.

(2.7)

We can simplify
\displaystyle{\cal R}_{3}
and observe that

\displaystyle\displaystyle({\cal R}_{3})_{2}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=u_{1,x}\biggl(\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{1}}\gamma(u)\big(-f^{\prime}(u_{1})u_{1,x}+\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(u_{1})(u_{1,x})^{2}+\alpha_{1}(u_{1})u_{1,xx}\big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{2}}\gamma(u)(-\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{1}}g(u)u_{1,x}-\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{2}}g(u)u_{2,x}+(\beta(u)u_{1,x}+\alpha_{2}(u)u_{2,x})_{x}\big)\biggl).

(2.8)

We have in particular obtained the following system:

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &w_{1,t}+f_{1}^{\prime}(u_{1})w_{1,x}-\alpha_{1}(u_{1})w_{1,xx}=\big(B_{1}(u){\cal R}_{1}+P(u){\cal R}_{2}+{\cal R}_{3}-{\cal R}_{4}+P(u){\cal R}\big)_{1},\\
&w_{2,t}+\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{2}}g(u)w_{2,x}-\alpha_{2}(u)w_{2,xx}=\big(B_{1}(u){\cal R}_{1}+P(u){\cal R}_{2}+{\cal R}_{3}-{\cal R}_{4}+P(u){\cal R}\big)_{2}.\end{aligned}\end{cases}

(2.9)

As in
[
14
]
, we set
\displaystyle w_{1}(t,x)=\widetilde{w}_{1}(t,X_{1}(t,x))
,
\displaystyle w_{2}(t,x)=\widetilde{w}_{1}(t,X_{2}(t,x))
with
\displaystyle(X_{1})_{x}(t,x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{1}(u_{1}(t,x))}}
and
\displaystyle(X_{2})_{x}(t,x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{2}(u(t,x))}}
. It implies that for
\displaystyle i\in\{1,2\}
,

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} w_{i,t}(t,x)&=\widetilde{w}_{i,t}(t,X_{i}(t,x))+\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(t,X_{i}(t,x))X_{i,t}(t,x),\\
w_{i,x}(t,x)&=\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(t,X_{i}(t,x))\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{i}(u(t,x))}},\\
w_{i,xx}(t,x)&=\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,X_{i}(t,x))\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}(u(t,x))}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}(u(t,x))^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{1}}(u(t,x))u_{1,x}(t,x)\\
&+\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{2}}(u(t,x))u_{2,x}(t,x))\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(t,X_{i}(t,x)).\end{aligned}\end{cases}

(2.10)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\displaystyle w_{i,xxx}(t,x)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\widetilde{w}_{i,xxx}(t,X_{i}(t,x))\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}^{3/2}(u(t,x))}

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2\alpha^{2}_{i}(u(t,x))}\left(\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{1}}(u(t,x))u_{1,x}(t,x)+\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{2}}(u(t,x))u_{2,x}(t,x)\right)\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,X_{i}(t,x))

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\frac{3}{4\alpha^{5/2}_{i}(u(t,x))}\left(\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{1}}(u(t,x))u_{1,x}(t,x)+\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{2}}(u(t,x))u_{2,x}(t,x)\right)^{2}\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(t,X_{i}(t,x))

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\alpha^{3/2}_{i}(u(t,x))}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{1}^{2}}(u(t,x))u_{1,x}(t,x)+\frac{\partial^{2}\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{2}\partial u_{1}}(u(t,x))u_{2,x}(t,x)\right)u_{1,x}(t,x)\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(t,X_{i}(t,x))

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\alpha^{3/2}_{i}(u(t,x))}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{1}\partial u_{2}}(u(t,x))u_{1,x}(t,x)+\frac{\partial^{2}\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{2}^{2}}(u(t,x))u_{2,x}(t,x)\right)u_{2,x}(t,x)\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(t,X_{i}(t,x))

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\alpha^{3/2}_{i}(u(t,x))}\left(\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{1}}(u(t,x))u_{1,xx}(t,x)+\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{2}}(u(t,x))u_{2,xx}(t,x)\right)\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(t,X_{i}(t,x)).

(2.11)

Since
\displaystyle X_{i}
is invertible from
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}
to
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}
we obtain the following system on
\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{i}
,

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &\widetilde{w}_{1,t}+\frac{f^{\prime}_{1}(u_{1})}{\sqrt{\alpha_{1}(u_{1})}}(\cdot,X_{1}^{-1})\widetilde{w}_{1,x}-\widetilde{w}_{1,xx}\\
&\quad\quad\quad=-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{1}(u_{1}(\cdot,X_{1}^{-1}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\frac{\partial\alpha_{1}}{\partial u_{1}}(u(\cdot,X_{1}^{-1}))u_{1,x}(\cdot,X_{1}^{-1})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad+\frac{\partial\alpha_{1}}{\partial u_{2}}(u(\cdot,X_{1}^{-1})))u_{2,x}(\cdot,X_{1}^{-1})))\widetilde{w}_{1,x}+{\cal G}_{1}(\cdot,X_{1}^{-1})-\widetilde{w}_{1,x}X_{1,t}(\cdot,X_{1}^{-1}),\\
&\widetilde{w}_{2,t}+\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{2}}g(u)}{\sqrt{\alpha_{2}(u)}}(\cdot,X_{2}^{-1})\widetilde{w}_{2,x}-\widetilde{w}_{2,xx}\\
&\quad\quad\quad=-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{2}(u(\cdot,X_{2}^{-1}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\frac{\partial\alpha_{2}}{\partial u_{1}}(u(\cdot,X_{2}^{-1}))u_{1,x}(\cdot,X_{2}^{-1})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad+\frac{\partial\alpha_{2}}{\partial u_{2}}(u(\cdot,X_{2}^{-1})))u_{2,x}(\cdot,X_{2}^{-1})))\widetilde{w}_{2,x}+{\cal G}_{2}(\cdot,X_{2}^{-1})-\widetilde{w}_{2,x}X_{2,t}(\cdot,X_{2}^{-1}),\end{aligned}\end{cases}

(2.12)

where the term
\displaystyle\mathcal{G}
is defined as follows

{\cal G}=B_{1}(u){\cal R}_{1}+P(u){\cal R}_{2}+{\cal R}_{3}-{\cal R}_{4}+P(u){\cal R}.

(2.13)

We denote now respectively by
\displaystyle G_{1}
and
\displaystyle G_{2}
the fundamental solution of the two following equations

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &w_{t}+\frac{f^{\prime}_{1}(u^{*}_{1})}{\sqrt{\alpha_{1}(u^{*}_{1})}}w_{x}-w_{xx}=0,\\
&w_{t}+\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{2}}g(u^{*})}{\sqrt{\alpha_{2}(u^{*})}}w_{x}-w_{xx}=0.\end{aligned}\end{cases}

(2.14)

The functions
\displaystyle G_{i}
satisfy the following estimates (see
[
11
]
) for
\displaystyle\kappa>0
large enough

\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|G_{i}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\leq\kappa,\quad\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|G_{i,x}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\leq\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{t}},\quad\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|G_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\leq\frac{\kappa}{t}.

(2.15)

To have the parabolic estimates for (
1.11
), we define the following constants

\displaystyle\displaystyle\kappa_{A}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\tiny\left|u-u^{*}\right|\leq\bar{\delta}}\max\limits_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{cc}&1\leq k\leq 5,\\
&|\theta|\leq 5\end{array}}\left\{\left|f^{(k)}_{1}(u_{1})\right|,\left|\partial^{\theta}g(u)\right|,1\right\},

\displaystyle\displaystyle\kappa_{B}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\tiny\left|u-u^{*}\right|\leq\bar{\delta}}\max\limits_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{cc}&0\leq|\theta|\leq 5,\\
&0\leq k\leq 5,\\
&1\leq i\leq 2\end{array}}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{i}(u)}},\sqrt{\alpha_{i}(u)},|\alpha_{1}^{(k)}(u_{1})|,|\partial^{\theta}\alpha_{2}(u)|,\right.

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt\left.\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}(u)},|\partial^{\theta}(\gamma(u))|,|\partial^{\theta}(\beta(u))|\right\},

with
\displaystyle\bar{\delta}>0
such that the solution
\displaystyle u(t,x)
lives all along the time in the ball
\displaystyle B(u^{*},{\bar{\delta}}/{2})
which will be satisfied provided that
\displaystyle\mbox{Tot.Var.}(u(0,\cdot))
is chosen sufficiently small.

Now, we have the following result on the parabolic regularizing of
\displaystyle u_{1}
and
\displaystyle u_{2}
. This helps us to establish the smoothness of solution up to a small time
\displaystyle\hat{t}
.

Proposition 2.1
.

There exist
\displaystyle C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}>0
depending on
\displaystyle\kappa_{A},\kappa_{B},\kappa
such that if
\displaystyle u
is a solution to the equation (
1.11
) satisfying

\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{1,x}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{2,x}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\leq\delta_{0}\mbox{ for all }t\in[0,\hat{t}]\mbox{ where }\hat{t}\leq\frac{1}{(C_{4}\delta_{0})^{2}},

(2.16)

for
\displaystyle\delta_{0}
sufficiently small in terms of
\displaystyle\kappa_{A}
,
\displaystyle\kappa_{B}
and
\displaystyle\kappa
, then we have for
\displaystyle i\in\{1,2\}

\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\leq\frac{C_{1}\delta_{0}}{\sqrt{t}},\,\,\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{i,xxx}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\leq\frac{C_{2}\delta_{0}}{t}\mbox{ and }\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{i,xxxx}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\leq\frac{C_{3}\delta_{0}}{t^{3/2}}.

(2.17)

Proof.

We first prove (
2.17
) for smooth initial data. We argue by contradiction. To this end, first we assume that the conclusion (
2.17
) does not hold. Due to the assumption of smoothness of initial data, solution is smooth up to a small time and due to the continuity we can assume that there exists a time
\displaystyle t^{*}
such that (
2.17
) holds for
\displaystyle t\in[0,t^{*}]
and equality attains at
\displaystyle t=t^{*}
.

From (
2.12
), the solution
\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{i}
with
\displaystyle i\in\{1,2\}
can be represented as follows for
\displaystyle t>0
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(t,\cdot)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=G_{i,x}({t}/{2},\cdot)*\widetilde{w}_{i}({t}/{2},\cdot)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}G_{i,x}(t-s)*\Big[\alpha_{i}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{1}}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))u_{1,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{2}}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))u_{2,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})\Big)\widetilde{w}_{i,x}+{\cal G}_{i}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})+2{\cal A}_{i}-\widetilde{w}_{i,x}X_{i,t}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})\Big]ds,

where
\displaystyle{\cal A}_{1}
and
\displaystyle{\cal A}_{2}
are defined as follows

\begin{array}[]{rl}&{\cal A}_{1}=\bigg(\frac{f^{\prime}_{1}(u^{*}_{1})}{\sqrt{\alpha_{1}(u^{*}_{1})}}-\frac{f^{\prime}_{1}(u_{1})}{\sqrt{\alpha_{1}(u_{1})}}(\cdot,X_{1}^{-1})\bigg)\widetilde{w}_{1,x},\\
&{\cal A}_{2}=\bigg(\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{2}}g(u^{*})}{\sqrt{\alpha_{2}(u^{*})}}-\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{2}}g(u)}{\sqrt{\alpha_{2}(u)}}(\cdot,X_{2}^{-1})\bigg)\widetilde{w}_{2,x}.\end{array}

(2.18)

From (
2.15
), we deduce that for
\displaystyle t>0
,
\displaystyle C>0

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{i}({t}/{2},\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{t-s}}\bigg(\|\Big[\alpha_{i}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{1}}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))u_{1,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\frac{\partial\alpha_{2}}{\partial u_{2}}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))u_{2,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})\Big)\Big]\widetilde{w}_{i,x}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\|{\cal G}_{i}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})\|_{L^{1}}+\|{\cal A}_{i}\|_{L^{1}}+\|\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(s,\cdot)X_{i,t}(s,X_{i}^{-1}(s,\cdot))\|_{L^{1}}\bigg)ds.

(2.19)

We observe using (
2.13
), (
2.8
),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|{\cal G}_{i}(s,X_{i}^{-1}(s,\cdot))\|_{L^{1}}=\mathcal{O}(1)\|{\cal G}_{i}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\big(\|u_{2,x}u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,x}u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,x}^{3}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,x}u_{2,x}^{2}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\|u_{2,x}u_{1,x}^{2}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}u_{1,x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}u_{2,x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,x}^{2}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\|u_{1,x}u_{2,x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)(\|u_{2,xx}\|_{L^{1}}\|u_{1,xx}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,xx}\|^{2}_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,xx}\|_{L^{1}}^{2}\delta_{0}+\delta_{0}\|u_{2,xx}\|^{2}_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\delta_{0}\|u_{2,xx}\|_{L^{1}}\|u_{1,xx}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}\|^{2}_{L^{1}}+\delta_{0}\|u_{1,xx}\|_{L^{1}}+\delta_{0}\|u_{2,xx}\|_{L^{1}}\big).

(2.20)

Similarly, we have since
\displaystyle w_{1}=u_{1,x}
and
\displaystyle w_{2}=-\gamma(u)u_{1,x}+u_{2,x}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|{\cal A}_{i}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\|u_{x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\|u_{x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|w_{i,x}(s,X_{i}^{-1}(s,\cdot)))\sqrt{\alpha_{i}(u(s,X_{i}^{-1}(s,\cdot))}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\|u_{x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|w_{i,x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\|u_{x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}(\|u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\|u_{1,x}^{2}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,x}u_{2,x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\delta_{0}(\|u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}).

(2.21)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\alpha_{i}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{1}}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))u_{1,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})+\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{2}}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))u_{2,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})\Big)\widetilde{w}_{i,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)(\|u_{1,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u_{2,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\big(\|u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}})(\|u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\|u_{1,x}^{2}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,x}u_{2,x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)(\|u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}})^{2}.

(2.22)

We want to estimate now
\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{i,x}X_{i,t}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})
, let us compute
\displaystyle X_{i,t}
, we have then:

\displaystyle\displaystyle X_{i,t}(x)=-\frac{1}{2}\int^{x}_{0}\alpha_{i}(u(t,z))^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{1}}\alpha_{i}(u(t,z))u_{1,t}(t,z)+\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{2}}\alpha_{i}(u(t,z))u_{2,t}(t,z))dz

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int^{x}_{0}\alpha_{i}(u(t,z))^{-\frac{3}{2}}\Big[\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{1}}\alpha_{i}(u(t,z))\big(-f^{\prime}(u_{1}(t,z))u_{1,x}(t,z)+(\alpha_{1}(u_{1})u_{1,x})_{x}(t,z)\big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{2}}\alpha_{i}(u(t,z))\big(-(g(u))_{x}(t,z)+(\beta(u)u_{1,x}+\alpha_{2}(u)u_{2,x})_{x}(t,z)\big)\Big]\,dz.

(2.23)

It implies that for
\displaystyle C_{2}>0
,

\displaystyle\|X_{i,t}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\|u_{1,x}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,x}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u^{2}_{1,x}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,x}u_{1,x}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u^{2}_{2,x}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle+\|u_{1,xx}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}\|_{L^{1}}\Big)(s,\cdot)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)(2\delta_{0}+2\|u_{1,xx}\|_{L^{1}}+2\|u_{2,xx}\|_{L^{1}})(s,\cdot).

It implies that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\widetilde{w}_{i,x}X_{i,t}(s,X_{i}^{-1}(s,\cdot))\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(2\delta_{0}+2\delta_{0}\|u_{1,xx}\|_{L^{1}}+\delta_{0}\|u_{2,xx}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,xx}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}\|_{L^{1}}\Big)(s,\cdot)\|\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\big(\delta_{0}+\|u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\big)\big(\|u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\big).

(2.24)

We wish now to estimate
\displaystyle\|u_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}
, we recall that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|u_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\leq\kappa_{B}^{2}(\|w_{1,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|w_{2,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|w^{2}_{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|w_{1}w_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{B}^{4}(\|\widetilde{w}_{1,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|\widetilde{w}_{2,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}})+\kappa_{B}^{2}\|u_{1,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+2\kappa^{3}_{B}\|u_{1,x}\|_{L^{1}}\big(\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{B}^{4}(\|\widetilde{w}_{1,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|\widetilde{w}_{2,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}})+3\kappa_{B}^{3}\delta_{0}\big(\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|u_{2,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\big).

(2.25)

It yields

(1-6\kappa_{B}^{3}\delta_{0})\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|u_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\leq 2\kappa_{B}^{4}(\|\widetilde{w}_{1,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|\widetilde{w}_{2,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}).

(2.26)

Since
\displaystyle 1-6\kappa_{B}^{3}\delta_{0}\geq\frac{1}{2}
we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|u_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\leq 4\kappa_{B}^{4}(\|\widetilde{w}_{1,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|\widetilde{w}_{2,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}).

(2.27)

Combining (
2.19
), (
2.20
), (
2.21
), (
2.22
), (
2.24
), (
2.27
) and (
2.17
) it gives for
\displaystyle C_{3}>0
large enough

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|u_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\kappa\kappa_{B}^{6}\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{t}}\Big(\|u_{1,x}({t}/{2},\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,x}({t}/{2},\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+C_{3}\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t-s}}\Big[\delta_{0}(\|u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt+\|u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}+\|u_{2,xx}(s,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\|u_{1,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|u_{2,xx}(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\Big]ds.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|u_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa\kappa_{B}^{4}\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{t}}\delta_{0}+4C_{3}\kappa_{B}^{4}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{t-s}}\kappa_{B}^{7}\kappa_{A}^{2}(2\delta_{0}^{2}\frac{2\kappa\kappa_{B}^{4}}{\sqrt{s}}+\frac{12\kappa^{2}\kappa_{B}^{8}\delta_{0}^{2}}{s})ds

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa\kappa_{B}^{6}8\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{t}}\delta_{0}+32C_{3}\kappa_{B}^{15}\kappa^{2}\kappa_{A}^{2}\delta_{0}^{2}+\frac{96\sqrt{2}C_{3}\kappa^{3}\kappa_{B}^{19}\kappa_{A}^{2}\delta_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{t}},

(2.28)

which implies since
\displaystyle\delta_{0}<\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{2\sqrt{2}(2-\sqrt{2})}{96C_{3}\kappa_{B}^{1}3\kappa^{2}\kappa_{A}^{2}}\right\}
and
\displaystyle t^{*}\leq\hat{t}
that

\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}(t^{*},\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}<\frac{16\kappa\kappa_{B}^{6}\delta_{0}}{\sqrt{t^{*}}}.

This contradicts with the assumption that equality holds in (
2.17
) at
\displaystyle t=t^{*}
. Hence, by density argument we conclude that (
2.17
) holds for BV initial data as well.

We apply similar arguments to prove the second and third estimate of (
2.17
), we have by using (
2.10
),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|u_{i,xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\|w_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|w_{2,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|w^{3}_{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|w^{2}_{1}w_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\|w_{1}w^{2}_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|w_{1,x}w_{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|w_{2,x}w_{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\|w_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|w_{2,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}\|w_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|w_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|w_{2,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\|w_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|w_{2,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|u_{2,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|u_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{i,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt+\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}+\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|u_{2,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|u_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)^{2}+\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\|u_{1,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|u_{2,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|u_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)^{2}\right).

Again by contradiction we assume that the second inequality in (
2.17
) is not satisfied, there exists again
\displaystyle t^{*}<\hat{t}
such that this estimate is a equality.
From the first inequality in (
2.17
), we deduce in particular that for
\displaystyle t\in]0,t^{*}]
we have for
\displaystyle C_{4}
large enough

\|u_{i,xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\leq{C}_{4}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\frac{\kappa^{2}\kappa_{B}^{12}\delta_{0}^{2}}{t}\bigg).

(2.29)

It remains now to estimate
\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}
, again we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=G_{i,x}({t}/{2},\cdot)*\widetilde{w}_{i,x}({t}/{2},\cdot)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}G_{i,x}(t-s)*\Big[{\cal H}_{i}\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}+{\cal H}_{i,x}\widetilde{w}_{i,x}+{\cal G}_{i,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})_{x}+{\cal A}_{i,x}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}X_{i,t}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})-\widetilde{w}_{i,x}X_{i,tx}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})_{x}\Big]ds,

(2.30)

with

{\cal H}_{i}=\alpha_{i}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{1}}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))u_{1,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})+\frac{\partial\alpha_{i}}{\partial u_{2}}(u(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))u_{2,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1}))\right].

It implies in particular using (
2.17
) that for
\displaystyle{C}_{5}>0
large enough

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{5}\delta_{0}}{t}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\left\|G_{i,x}(t-s)*\big[{\cal H}_{i}\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}+{\cal H}_{i,x}\widetilde{w}_{i,x}+{\cal A}_{i,x}\right.

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt+{\cal G}_{i,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})_{x}-\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}X_{i,t}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\left.-\widetilde{w}_{i,x}X_{i,tx}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})_{x}\big]\right\|_{L^{1}}ds.

(2.31)

We observe that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right),

(2.32)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

(2.33)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{3}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\Big),

(2.34)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{1}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right),

(2.35)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{1,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

(2.36)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{3}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\Big),

(2.37)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{2}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}},

(2.38)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{2,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\Big),

(2.39)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{3}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right),

(2.40)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{3,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

(2.41)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{3}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\Big),

(2.42)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{4}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}},

(2.43)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{4,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\Big).

(2.44)

We note now that using (
2.17
)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|{\cal G}_{i,x}(t,X_{i}^{-1}(t,\cdot))(X_{i}^{-1})_{x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}=\mathcal{O}(1)\|{\cal G}_{i,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\big[\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{1}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{1,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{2}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{2,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{3,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{4,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\big]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{3}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t}+\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t^{3/2}}\right).

(2.45)

Similarly we have using (
2.10
) and (
2
),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)X_{i,t}(t,X_{i}^{-1}(t,\cdot))\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|X_{i,t}(t,X_{i}^{-1}(t,\cdot))\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\delta_{0}+\sum_{l=1}^{2}\|u_{l,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)\left(\|w_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\sum_{l=1}^{2}\|u_{l,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\|w_{i,x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\delta_{0}+\|u_{xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)\Big[\|u_{xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|u_{xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\|u_{x}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|u_{xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}^{2}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}(t,\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\delta_{0}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\sqrt{t}}\right)\left(\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{2}\|u_{l,xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right).

(2.46)

Taking derivative of (
2.23
) with respect to
\displaystyle x
we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle X_{i,tx}(x)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{i}(u(t,x))^{-\frac{3}{2}}\Big[\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{1}}\alpha_{i}(u(t,x))\big(-f^{\prime}(u_{1}(t,x))u_{1,x}(t,x)+(\alpha_{1}(u_{1})u_{1,x})_{x}(t,x)\big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{2}}\alpha_{i}(u(t,x))\big(-(g(u))_{x}(t,x)+(\beta(u)u_{1,x}+\alpha_{2}(u)u_{2,x})_{x}(t,x)\big)\Big].

For sufficiently large constant
\displaystyle C_{10},C_{11}>0
we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|X_{i,tx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big[\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{1,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{1,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{1,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{1,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{2,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{2,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{2,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{2,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left[\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right].

It yields for large enough constants
\displaystyle C_{12},C_{13},C_{14}>0
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{i,x}X_{i,tx}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|w_{i,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|X_{i,tx}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|(X_{i}^{-1})_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|w_{i,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|X_{i,tx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|X_{i,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{2}\|u_{l,xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{1,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{2,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{2}\|u_{l,xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{t}}.

(2.47)

From the definition of
\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{i}
we get

\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{H}_{i}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\mbox{ and }\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{H}_{i,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}},

for some large constant
\displaystyle C_{15}>0
. Hence, it follows for
\displaystyle C_{16},C_{17}>0
large enough,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|{\cal H}_{i}\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|{\cal H}_{i,x}\widetilde{w}_{i,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{H}_{i}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{H}_{i,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{i,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|w_{i,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left(\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{2}\|u_{l,xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{2}\|u_{l,xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)\frac{\delta_{0}}{\sqrt{t}}.

(2.48)

Similarly, from (
2.18
) we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{A}_{i,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left(\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{2}\|u_{l,xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{2}\|u_{l,xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\right)\frac{\delta_{0}}{\sqrt{t}}.

(2.49)

From (
2.29
), (
2.31
), (
2.45
), (
2.46
), (
2.47
), (
2.48
) and (
2.49
) we get for
\displaystyle C_{6}>0
large enough

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|u_{i,xxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{C_{2}\delta_{0}}{2t}+C_{6}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t-s}}\left(\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{s}+\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{s^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)ds\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\big(\frac{C_{2}\delta_{0}}{2t}+\frac{2\sqrt{2}C_{6}\delta_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{t}}+\frac{4C_{6}\delta_{0}^{2}}{t})\leq\frac{C_{2}}{t},

(2.50)

if we take
\displaystyle C_{2}\geq 16C_{6}
sufficiently large and such that
\displaystyle\delta_{0}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{t}}}
which gives the result.

We apply similar arguments to prove the fourth order estimate of
\displaystyle u_{xxxx}
as in (
2.17
). From (
2.6
) and (
2.11
) we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|u_{i,xxxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\bigg(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|w_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\bigg(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|w_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}^{3}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\bigg(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{1,xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{2,xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{1,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{2,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2})(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{1,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{2,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}^{3}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\bigg).

From (
2.10
) we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{i,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|{w}_{i,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}=\mathcal{O}(1)(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|w_{i,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|w_{i,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right).

Then we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|u_{i,xxxx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\bigg(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{1,xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{2,xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2})\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}^{3}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\bigg(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{1,xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{2,xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t^{3/2}}\bigg).

(2.51)

Similarly, we have

\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\widetilde{w}_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}}{t^{3/2}}\right).

To estimate
\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}
, again we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{i,xxx}(t,\cdot)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=G_{i,x}({t}/{2},\cdot)*\widetilde{w}_{i,xxx}({t}/{2},\cdot)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}G_{i,x}(t-s)*\Big[{\cal H}_{i}\widetilde{w}_{i,xxx}+2{\cal H}_{i,x}\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}+{\cal H}_{i,xx}\widetilde{w}_{i,x}+2{\cal A}_{i,xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+{\cal G}_{i,xx}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})^{2}_{x}+{\cal G}_{i,x}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})_{xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\widetilde{w}_{i,xxx}X_{i,t}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})-2\widetilde{w}_{i,xx}X_{i,tx}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})_{x}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt-\widetilde{w}_{i,x}X_{i,txx}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})^{2}_{x}-\widetilde{w}_{i,x}X_{i,tx}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})_{xx}\Big]ds.

(2.52)

We calculate

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\cal G}_{xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=u_{xx}\cdot DB_{1}(u){\cal R}_{1}+(u_{x}\otimes u_{x}):D^{2}B_{1}(u){\cal R}_{1}+2u_{x}\cdot DB_{1}(u){\cal R}_{1,x}+B_{1}(u){\cal R}_{1,xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+u_{xx}\cdot DP(u){\cal R}_{2}+(u_{x}\otimes u_{x}):D^{2}P(u){\cal R}_{2}+2u_{x}\cdot DP(u){\cal R}_{2,x}+P(u){\cal R}_{2,xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+{\cal R}_{3,xx}-{\cal R}_{4,xx}+u_{xx}\cdot DP(u){\cal R}+(u_{x}\otimes u_{x}):D^{2}P(u){\cal R}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+2u_{x}\cdot DP(u){\cal R}_{x}+P(u){\cal R}_{xx}.

Note that

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\cal R}_{1,xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
2\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xxxx}+4\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xxx}+2\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial x^{3}}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xx}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial x^{4}}(\gamma(u))u_{1,x}+2\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial x^{3}}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xx}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial^{2}x}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xxx}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{x}\right|\left|u_{1,xxxx}\right|+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{1,xxx}\right|+\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}\left|u_{1,xxx}\right|+\left|u_{xxx}\right|\left|u_{1,xx}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|\left|u_{1,xx}\right|+\left|u_{x}\right|^{3}\left|u_{1,xx}\right|+\left|u_{xxxx}\right|\left|u_{1,x}\right|+\left|u_{xxx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|\left|u_{1,x}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}\left|u_{1,x}\right|+\left|u_{xx}\right|^{2}\left|u_{1,x}\right|+\left|u_{x}\right|^{4}\left|u_{1,x}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\cal R}_{2,xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}(u_{1})(u_{1,x}^{2}+u_{1,xx})u_{1,x}u_{1,xx}+2\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{1}(u_{1})u_{1,x}[u_{1,x}u_{1,xxx}+u_{1,xx}^{2}]\\
\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial x^{3}}(\beta(u))u_{1,xx}+\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial x^{3}}(\alpha_{2}(u))u_{2,xx}+2\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}(\beta(u))u_{1,xxx}+2\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}(\alpha_{2}(u))u_{2,xxx}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(u_{1})[3u_{1,xxx}u_{1,xx}+u_{1,x}u_{1,xxxx}]+\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(u_{1})[3u_{1,xxx}u_{1,xx}+u_{1,x}u_{1,xxxx}]\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\beta(u))u_{1,xxxx}+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\alpha_{2}(u))u_{2,xxxx}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{x}\right|\left|u_{xxxx}\right|+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{xxx}\right|+\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}\left|u_{xxx}\right|+\left|u_{xx}\right|^{2}\left|u_{x}\right|+\left|u_{x}\right|^{3}\left|u_{xx}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\cal R}_{3,xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial t\partial x^{2}}(\gamma(u))u_{1,x}+2\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial x}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xx}+\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xxx}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{txx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|+\left|u_{tx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}+\left|u_{t}\right|\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|+\left|u_{t}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{3}+\left|u_{tx}\right|\left|u_{xx}\right|+\left|u_{t}\right|\left|u_{xxx}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{xxxx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|+\left|u_{xxx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}+\left|u_{xx}\right|^{2}\left|u_{x}\right|+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{3}+\left|u_{x}\right|^{4}+\left|u_{xxx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}+\left|u_{x}\right|^{3}+\left|u_{xxx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{3}+\left|u_{x}\right|^{5}+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}+\left|u_{x}\right|^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{xx}\right|^{2}\left|u_{x}\right|+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{3}+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{3}+\left|u_{x}\right|^{5}++\left|u_{x}\right|^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\left|u_{xxx}\right|\left|u_{xx}\right|+\left|u_{xx}\right|^{2}\left|u_{x}\right|+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{3}+\left|u_{xx}\right|^{2}+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}+\left|u_{x}\right|\left|u_{xxx}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{xxx}\right|+\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}\left|u_{xxx}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\cal R}_{4,xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=[u_{x}\otimes u_{x}:D^{2}A_{1}(u)+u_{xx}\cdot DA_{1}(u)]\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\gamma(u))u_{1,x}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+u_{x}\cdot DA_{1}(u)\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}(\gamma(u))u_{1,x}+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xx}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+A_{1}(u)\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial x^{3}}(\gamma(u))u_{1,x}+2\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xx}+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\gamma(u))u_{1,xxx}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{x}\right|^{4}+\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}\left|u_{xx}\right|+\left|u_{xxx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|+\left|u_{xx}\right|^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\cal R}_{xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}(\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(u_{1})(u_{1,x})^{2})_{xxx}-\left(f^{\prime\prime}(u_{1})(u_{1,x})^{2}\right)_{xx}\\
\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\beta(u))u_{1,x}\right)_{xxx}+\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\alpha_{2}(u))u_{2,x}\right)_{xxx}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u_{x}\right|^{5}+\left|u_{xx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{3}+\left|u_{xxx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|^{2}+\left|u_{xxxx}\right|\left|u_{x}\right|+\left|u_{xxx}\right|\left|u_{xx}\right|+\left|u_{x}\right|\left|u_{xx}\right|^{2}\right).

Subsequently, we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|{\cal G}_{i,xx}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})^{2}_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{1}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{1}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{1,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{1,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{2}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{2}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{2,x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{2,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{3,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\mathcal{R}_{4,xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right).

Then we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|{\cal G}_{i,xx}(\cdot,X_{i}^{-1})(X_{i}^{-1})^{2}_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\right)\left[\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{3}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|^{4}_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|u_{xxx}\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{x}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}+\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xxx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u_{xx}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{\infty}}\right)
