Now we are going to estimate the new terms compared to
[
3
]

\displaystyle\left|h_{1,xx}v_{1}-h_{1}v_{1,xx}\right|
and
\displaystyle\left|h_{1,xx}w_{1}-h_{1}w_{1,xx}\right|
. We recall that
\displaystyle\hat{h}_{1}=\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1}
. Then
\displaystyle h_{1,t}=\hat{h}_{1,x}
. This implies

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{h}_{1,t}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}h_{1,tx}+u_{1,t}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}h_{1,x}-(\lambda_{1}-v_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime})h_{1,t}-(\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-v_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime})u_{1,t}h_{1}+v_{1,t}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}h_{1}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}-(\lambda_{1}-v_{1}\alpha^{\prime}_{1})\hat{h}_{1})_{x}-\alpha_{1}^{\prime}v_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}+u_{1,t}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}h_{1,x}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+(\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-v_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime})v_{1}\hat{h}_{1}-(\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-v_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime})u_{1,t}h_{1}+v_{1,t}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}h_{1}-v_{1,x}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}\hat{h}_{1}.

Since
\displaystyle u_{1,t}=w_{1}
and
\displaystyle v_{1,t}=w_{1,x}
we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{h}_{1,t}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{h}_{1})_{x}-\alpha_{1}^{\prime}v_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}+w_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}h_{1,x}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+(\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-v_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime})v_{1}\hat{h}_{1}-(\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-v_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime})w_{1}h_{1}+w_{1,x}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}h_{1}-v_{1,x}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}\hat{h}_{1}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{h}_{1})_{x}-\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(v_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}-v_{1,x}\hat{h}_{1})+2\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(w_{1}h_{1,x}-v_{1,x}\hat{h}_{1})

\displaystyle\displaystyle+(\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-v_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime})(v_{1}(\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1})-(\alpha_{1}v_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}v_{1})h_{1})

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(w_{1,x}h_{1}-w_{1}h_{1,x})

\displaystyle\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{h}_{1})_{x}-\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(v_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}-v_{1,x}\hat{h}_{1})+\alpha_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-v_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime})(v_{1}h_{1,x}-v_{1,x}h_{1})

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(w_{1,x}h_{1}-w_{1}h_{1,x})+2\alpha_{1}^{\prime}((\alpha_{1}v_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}v_{1})h_{1,x}-v_{1,x}(\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1}))

\displaystyle\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{h}_{1})_{x}-\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(v_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}-v_{1,x}\hat{h}_{1})+\alpha_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-v_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime})(v_{1}h_{1,x}-v_{1,x}h_{1})

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(w_{1,x}h_{1}-w_{1}h_{1,x})+2\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(h_{1}v_{1,x}-h_{1,x}v_{1}).

It implies that
\displaystyle\hat{h}_{1}
satisfies the following equation

\hat{h}_{1,t}+(\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{h}_{1})_{x}-(\alpha_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x})_{x}=\hat{\varphi}_{1},

(7.28)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}_{1}=

\displaystyle-\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(v_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}-v_{1,x}\hat{h}_{1})+\alpha_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-v_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime})(v_{1}h_{1,x}-v_{1,x}h_{1})+\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(w_{1,x}h_{1}-w_{1}h_{1,x})

(7.29)

\displaystyle+2\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(h_{1}v_{1,x}-h_{1,x}v_{1}).

Now we can calculate

\displaystyle h_{1,xx}v_{1}-h_{1}v_{1,xx}=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\big(\alpha_{1}h_{1,xx}v_{1}-h_{1}\alpha_{1}v_{1,xx}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\big(\hat{h}_{1,x}v_{1}+\tilde{\lambda}_{1,x}h_{1}v_{1}+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1,x}v_{1}-h_{1}w_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1,x}v_{1}h_{1}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1}v_{1,x}-\alpha^{\prime}_{1}v_{1}(v_{1}h_{1,x}-h_{1}v_{1,x})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\left(\hat{h}_{1,x}v_{1}-\hat{h}_{1}v_{1,x}+h_{1,x}w_{1}-h_{1}w_{1,x}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\left(\hat{h}_{1}v_{1,x}-h_{1,x}w_{1}+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1,x}v_{1}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1}v_{1,x}-\alpha^{\prime}_{1}v_{1}(v_{1}h_{1,x}-h_{1}v_{1,x})\right).

Then we can simplify

\displaystyle h_{1,xx}v_{1}-h_{1}v_{1,xx}=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\big(\alpha_{1}h_{1,xx}v_{1}-h_{1}\alpha_{1}v_{1,xx}\big)

(7.30)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\left(\hat{h}_{1,x}v_{1}-\hat{h}_{1}v_{1,x}+h_{1,x}w_{1}-h_{1}w_{1,x}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\left(\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}v_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1}v_{1,x}-\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}v_{1,x}+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1,x}v_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\left(\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1,x}v_{1}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1}v_{1,x}-\alpha^{\prime}_{1}v_{1}(v_{1}h_{1,x}-h_{1}v_{1,x})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\left((\hat{h}_{1,x}v_{1}-\hat{h}_{1}v_{1,x})+(h_{1,x}w_{1}-h_{1}w_{1,x})\right)+(\frac{2\tilde{\lambda}_{1}}{\alpha_{1}}-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}v_{1}}{\alpha_{1}})\left(h_{1,x}v_{1}-h_{1}v_{1,x}\right).

Therefore, we have applying Lemma
5.1
to the couples
\displaystyle(\hat{h}_{1},v_{1})
,
\displaystyle(h_{1},w_{1})
and
\displaystyle(v_{1},h_{1})
and using the Lemma
7.11
we get

\int_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h_{1,xx}v_{1}-h_{1}v_{1,xx}|dxds=\mathcal{O}(1)\delta_{0}^{2}.

(7.31)

Similarly we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle h_{1,xx}w_{1}-h_{1}w_{1,xx}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\left((\hat{h}_{1,x}w_{1}-\hat{h}_{1}w_{1,x})+(h_{1,x}z_{1}-h_{1}z_{1,x})\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+(\frac{2\tilde{\lambda}_{1}}{\alpha_{1}}-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}v_{1}}{\alpha_{1}})\left(h_{1,x}w_{1}-h_{1}w_{1,x}\right).

Again using Lemma
5.1
to the couples
\displaystyle(\hat{h}_{1},w_{1})
,
\displaystyle(h_{1},z_{1})
and
\displaystyle(h_{1},w_{1})
and using the Lemma
7.11
we get

\int_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h_{1,xx}w_{1}-h_{1}w_{1,xx}|dxds=\mathcal{O}(1)\delta_{0}^{2}.

(7.32)

It remains now to deal with the terms
\displaystyle|h_{1,x}|^{2}\chi_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\geq\delta_{1}\}}
,
\displaystyle|(w_{1}-\sigma_{1}v_{1})(|h_{1}|+|h_{1,x}|)
which as in the previous section required energy estimate. To do this we multiply again the equation (
7.13
) by
\displaystyle h_{1}\hat{\theta}(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}})
with
\displaystyle\hat{\theta}
defined as in (
6.8
) and we integrate. We obtain then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\biggl((\frac{h_{1}^{2}\hat{\theta}}{2})_{t}-\frac{h_{1}^{2}}{2}(\hat{\theta}_{t}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x}-(\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x})_{x})+2\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}h_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x}+\hat{\theta}h_{1,x}(\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1})\biggl)dx=0.

It implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\theta}h_{1,x}(\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1})

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\frac{h_{1}^{2}\hat{\theta}}{2})_{t}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{h_{1}^{2}}{2}(\hat{\theta}_{t}+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x}-(\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x})_{x})dx

(7.33)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}2\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}h_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{h_{1}^{2}}{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x}.

A direct computation gives using the equation (
7.28
)

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{t}+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x}-(\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x})_{x}=\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\frac{\hat{h}_{1,t}}{h_{1}}-\frac{h_{1,t}\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}^{2}})+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(\frac{\hat{h}_{1,x}}{h_{1}}-\frac{h_{1,x}\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}^{2}})

(7.34)

\displaystyle-\alpha^{\prime}_{1}v_{1}\hat{\theta}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}-\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}^{2}+2\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}^{\prime}\frac{h_{1,x}}{h_{1}}\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}-\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\frac{\hat{h}_{1,xx}h_{1}-h_{1,xx}\hat{h}_{1}}{v_{1}^{2}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{\theta}^{\prime}}{h_{1}}(\hat{h}_{1,t}+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x}-(\alpha_{1}\hat{h}_{1,x})_{x})-\frac{\hat{\theta}^{\prime}\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}^{2}}(h_{1,t}+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1,x}-(\alpha_{1}h_{1,x})_{x})-\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}^{\prime}\frac{h_{1,x}}{h_{1}}\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{\theta}^{\prime}}{h_{1}}\hat{\varphi}_{1}-\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}^{2}+2\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x}\frac{h_{1,x}}{h_{1}}.

Plugging this last expression in (
7.33
), it implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\theta}h_{1,x}(\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1})

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\frac{h_{1}^{2}\hat{\theta}}{2})_{t}dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\alpha_{1}\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime}h_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}^{2}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\alpha_{1}\frac{h_{1,x}}{h_{1}}h_{1}^{2}\hat{\theta}_{x}

(7.35)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{h_{1}^{2}}{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\hat{\theta}_{x}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\theta}^{\prime}h_{1}\hat{\varphi}_{1}.

We observe that
\displaystyle\hat{\theta}(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}})\neq 0
if

\displaystyle|\hat{h}_{1}|=|\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}h_{1}|\geq\frac{3\delta_{1}}{5}|h_{1}|
which gives using (
6.12
)

|\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}|\geq\frac{3\delta_{1}}{5}|h_{1}|-\|\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}|h_{1}|\geq 2\|\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}|h_{1}|.

(7.36)

From (
7.35
), (
7.36
), (
1.9
), (
7.29
), (
7.30
) we get for
\displaystyle t\in[\hat{t},T]

\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\theta h_{1,x}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{h_{1}^{2}\theta}{2}\right)_{t}dx+O(1)\int_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\leq\delta_{1}\}}h_{1}^{2}|\theta^{\prime\prime}|\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}^{2}dx

(7.37)

\displaystyle+O(1)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+|\frac{h_{1,x}}{h_{1}}|\chi_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\leq\frac{4\delta_{1}}{5}\}})|\hat{h}_{1,x}h_{1}-h_{1,x}\hat{h}_{1}|dx

\displaystyle+O(1)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(|v_{1}h_{1,xx}-v_{1,x}h_{1,xx}|+|v_{1}h_{1,x}-v_{1,x}h_{1}|\big)dx

\displaystyle+O(1)\int_{\mathbb{R}}|w_{1,x}h_{1}-w_{1}h_{1,x}|\,dx.

It remains now to estimate the term
\displaystyle\int_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\leq\delta_{1}\}}h_{1}^{2}|\theta^{\prime\prime}|\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}^{2}dx
, as previously we set:

{\cal L}_{1}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sqrt{h_{1}^{2}+\hat{h}_{1}^{2}}(t,x)dx.

As previously we can prove that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\cal L}_{1}(t)\leq-\int\frac{\alpha_{1}|h_{1}||\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}|^{2}}{(1+\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}dx+\|\hat{\varphi}_{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}.

We deduce then that from (
7.29
), (
7.27
), (
7.32
) and Lemma
7.11
, we have

\int_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\leq\delta_{1}\}}h_{1}^{2}|\theta^{\prime\prime}|\left(\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right)_{x}^{2}dx=O(1)\delta_{0}^{2}.

(7.38)

Combining now (
7.37
), (
7.38
), (
7.27
) and the Lemma
5.1

applied to the couple
\displaystyle(h_{1},\hat{h}_{1})
we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\geq\frac{4\delta_{1}}{5}\}}h_{1,x}^{2}dxds=O(1)\delta_{0}^{2}.

(7.39)

We can now estimate the term
\displaystyle(|h_{1,x}|+|h_{1}|)(w_{1}+\sigma_{1}v_{1})
. First we observe that this term is different from zero if
\displaystyle|\frac{w_{1}}{v_{1}}|\geq\delta_{1}
then we have:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}}\left|w_{1}+\sigma_{1}v_{1}\right|(|h_{1}|+\left|h_{1,x}\right|)\,dxds

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\leq\frac{4\delta_{1}}{5}\}\cup\{|\frac{w_{1}}{w_{1}}|\geq\delta_{1}\}}\left|w_{1}+\sigma_{1}v_{1}\right|(|h_{1}|+\left|h_{1,x}\right|)dxds

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\geq\frac{4\delta_{1}}{5}\}\cup\{|\frac{w_{1}}{w_{1}}|\geq\delta_{1}\}}\left|w_{1}+\sigma_{1}v_{1}\right|(|h_{1}|+\left|h_{1,x}\right|)dxds.

First we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\geq\frac{4\delta_{1}}{5}\}\cup\{|\frac{w_{1}}{w_{1}}|\geq\delta_{1}\}}\left|w_{1}+\sigma_{1}v_{1}\right|(|h_{1}|+\left|h_{1,x}\right|)dxds

(7.40)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=O(1)\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\geq\frac{4\delta_{1}}{5}\}}(h_{1,x})^{2}dxds+O(1)\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\{|\frac{w_{1}}{w_{1}}|\geq\delta_{1}\}}|w_{1}+\sigma_{1}v_{1}|^{2}dxds

(7.41)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=O(1)\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\geq\frac{4\delta_{1}}{5}\}}(h_{1,x})^{2}dxds+O(1)\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\{|\frac{w_{1}}{w_{1}}|\geq\delta_{1}\}}(v_{1,x})^{2}dxds.

(7.42)

We observe now that
\displaystyle\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\leq\frac{4\delta_{1}}{5}\}\cup\{|\frac{w_{1}}{v_{1}}|\geq\delta_{1}\}
is included in
\displaystyle\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\leq\frac{4}{5}|\frac{w_{1}}{v_{1}}|\}\cup\{|\alpha_{1}h_{1,x}|\leq\delta_{1}|h_{1}|\}
provided that
\displaystyle\delta_{0}
is sufficiently small in terms of
\displaystyle\delta_{1}
then we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|h_{1,x}v_{1}-h_{1}v_{1,x}\right|=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\left|\hat{h}_{1}v_{1}-w_{1}h_{1}\right|=\frac{\left|h_{1}v_{1}\right|}{\alpha_{1}}\left|\frac{w_{1}}{v_{1}}-\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}\right|\geq\frac{\left|h_{1}w_{1}\right|}{5\alpha_{1}}\geq\frac{\left|h_{1,x}w_{1}\right|}{5\delta_{1}}\geq\frac{\left|h_{1,x}v_{1}\right|}{5}

Therefore we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int_{\{|\frac{\hat{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}|\leq\frac{4\delta_{1}}{5}\}\cup\{|\frac{w_{1}}{w_{1}}|\geq\delta_{1}\}}\left|w_{1}+\sigma_{1}v_{1}\right|(|h_{1}|+\left|h_{1,x}\right|)dxds

\displaystyle\displaystyle=O(1)\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int\left|h_{1,x}v_{1}-h_{1}v_{1,x}\right|dxds

\displaystyle\displaystyle=O(1)\delta_{0}^{2}.

(7.43)

Combining (
7.39
), (
7.42
), (
7.43
) and (
6.14
), we deduce that

\int\limits_{\hat{t}}^{T}\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}}\left|w_{1}+\sigma_{1}v_{1}\right|(|h_{1}|+\left|h_{1,x}\right|)\,dxds=O(1)\delta_{0}^{2}.

(7.44)

From (
7.26
), (
7.27
), (
7.31
), (
7.32
) and (
7.44
) we deduce that
\displaystyle T_{*}=+\infty
. This completes the proof of (
1.15
).

We wish to conclude this section by proving the estimate (
1.16
) which will finish the proof of the Theorem
1.1
. From the Proposition
2.1
and the Corollary
2.2
, we have seen that there exists
\displaystyle C>0
large enough such that

\begin{cases}\|u_{xx}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}&\leq\frac{C\delta_{0}}{\sqrt{t}}\;\;\mbox{if}\;0<t\leq\hat{t},\\
&\leq\frac{C\delta_{0}}{\sqrt{\hat{t}}}\;\;\mbox{if}\;t\geq\hat{t}.\end{cases}

(7.45)

We deduce in particular that there exists
\displaystyle L_{3}>0
such that for any
\displaystyle t>0

\|u_{t}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\leq L_{3}(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{t}}).

(7.46)

For any
\displaystyle 0\leq s<t
we have then using (
7.46
)

\|u(t)-u(s)\|_{L^{1}}\leq\int^{t}_{s}\|u_{t}(\theta)\|_{L^{1}}d\theta\leq L_{3}(|t-s|+|\sqrt{t}-\sqrt{s}|).

8
Vanishing viscosity limit

As claimed in Corollary
1.3
we want to prove vanishing viscosity limit as
\displaystyle\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
for the following Cauchy problem

u^{\varepsilon}_{t}+A(u^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon}_{x}=\varepsilon(B(u^{\varepsilon})_{x})_{x}\mbox{ and }u^{\varepsilon}(0,x)=\bar{u}(x).

(8.1)

Note that if we define
\displaystyle u
as follows
\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=u(\frac{t}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon})
then
\displaystyle u
solves the following problem with fix viscosity but scaled initial data,

u_{t}+A(u)u_{x}=(B(u)_{x})_{x}\mbox{ and }u(0,x)=\bar{u}(\varepsilon x).

(8.2)

Observe that

\displaystyle\displaystyle TV(\bar{u}(\varepsilon\cdot))

\displaystyle\displaystyle=TV(\bar{u}(\cdot)),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\bar{u}(\varepsilon\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\bar{u}(\cdot)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}.

Therefore using the Theorem
1.1
, we obtain for
\displaystyle 0\leq s\leq t

\displaystyle\displaystyle TV(u^{\varepsilon}(t))

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq L_{1}TV(\bar{u}),

(8.3)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u^{\varepsilon}(t)-v^{\varepsilon}(t)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\varepsilon\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u(t)-v(t)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}\leq L_{2}\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|\bar{u}-\bar{v}\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}},

(8.4)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\hskip-0.56905pt\left|u^{\varepsilon}(t)-u^{\varepsilon}(s)\right|\hskip-0.56905pt\right|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq L_{3}\left(\left|t-s\right|+\sqrt{\varepsilon}\left|\sqrt{t}-\sqrt{s}\right|\right),.

(8.5)

The convergence of
\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}
as
\displaystyle\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
follows from a standard argument with an application of Helly’s theorem and the
\displaystyle L^{1}
continuity (
8.5
). Indeed, due to the uniform TV estimate (
8.3
) by using Helly’s theorem we can pass to a the limit (up to a subsequence) for a countable dense set
\displaystyle\{t_{n}\}
and then applying
\displaystyle L^{1}
continuity we can define the limit function at all time
\displaystyle t>0
. We set

L^{1}_{loc}-\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}u^{\varepsilon_{k}}(t,\cdot)=u(t,\cdot).

(8.6)

We wish no to prove that
\displaystyle u
is a global weak solution for the system (
1.1
). In particular up to a subsequence
\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
converges almost everywhere to
\displaystyle u
, it implies in particular that for any function
\displaystyle\varphi
in
\displaystyle C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R})
we have applying dominated convergence

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{x}(s,x)f_{1}(u_{1}^{\varepsilon}(s,x)dsdx\rightarrow_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{x}(s,x)f_{1}(u(s,x)dsdx

(8.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{x}(s,x)g(u^{\varepsilon}(s,x)dsdx\rightarrow_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{x}(s,x)g(u(s,x)dsdx.

Similarly we have for any
\displaystyle T>0

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|B(u^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon}_{x}|dxds\leq\sup_{\{|v-u^{*}|\leq 2\delta_{0}\}}|B(v)|\delta_{0}\varepsilon T,

(8.8)

then
\displaystyle\varepsilon B(u^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon}_{x}
converges to zero in
\displaystyle L^{1}_{loc,t,x}
. It implies that
\displaystyle u
is a global weak solution of (
1.1
). In addition as in
[
4
]
we can show that the solution
\displaystyle u
satisfies the Liu condition for any shock. Applying the result of
[
7
]
, it implies that the solution
\displaystyle u
of (
1.1
) is unique since it is a global weak solution small in
\displaystyle BV
and satisfying Liu shocks conditions. In particular it means that the sequence
\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
has a unique accumulation point and then the convergence of
\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
to
\displaystyle u
is strong in
\displaystyle L^{1}_{loc}
. It completes the proof of the Corollary
1.3
.

Appendix A
Viscous travelling wave

The viscous travelling wave
\displaystyle u(t,x)=U(x-\sigma t)
of the equation (
1.11
) satisfies

(A(U)-\sigma)U^{\prime}=(B(U)U^{\prime})^{\prime}.

We can write

U^{\prime\prime}=B^{-1}(U)(A(U)-\sigma)U^{\prime}-B^{-1}(U)(U^{\prime}\cdot DB(U))U^{\prime}.

We consider the following system of ordinary differential equations

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} \dot{u}&=v,\\
\dot{v}&=B^{-1}(u)(A(u)-\sigma)v-B^{-1}(u)(v\bullet DB(u))v,\\
\dot{\sigma}&=0.\end{aligned}\end{cases}

(A.1)

We note that
\displaystyle P^{*}_{i}:=(u^{*},0,\lambda_{i}(u^{*}))
are equilibrium points for
\displaystyle 1\leq i\leq 2
. We linearize near the point
\displaystyle P^{*}_{i}
and get

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} \dot{u}&=v,\\
\dot{v}&=B^{-1}(u^{*})(A(u^{*})-\lambda_{i}(u^{*}))v,\\
\dot{\sigma}&=0.\end{aligned}\end{cases}

(A.2)

Let
\displaystyle\{r_{i}(u)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
and
\displaystyle\{l_{i}(u)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
be the sets of right and left eigenvector of
\displaystyle A(u)
.We denote
\displaystyle r_{i}^{*}=r_{i}(u^{*}),l_{i}^{*}=l_{i}(u^{*})
. We define
\displaystyle V_{i},1\leq i\leq 2
as follows

v=\sum\limits_{j}V_{j}r_{j}^{*},\quad V_{j}:=l_{j}^{*}\cdot v.

(A.3)

Then we have

Z=DF(u^{*},0,\lambda_{i}(u^{*}))=\begin{pmatrix}O_{n}&I_{n}&0\\
O_{n}&B^{-1}(u^{*})(A(u^{*})-\lambda_{i}(u^{*})I_{n})&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}.

(A.4)

Subsequently, we get

Z^{2}=(DF(u^{*},0,\lambda_{i}(u^{*})))^{2}=\begin{pmatrix}O_{n}&A(u^{*})-\lambda_{i}(u^{*})I_{n}&0\\
O_{n}&(A(u^{*})-\lambda_{i}(u^{*})I_{n})^{2}&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}.

(A.5)

Therefore the center subspace will look like

\mathcal{N}_{i}:=\left\{(u,v,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R};\,V_{j}=0,j\neq i\right\}.

(A.6)

Note that
\displaystyle\mbox{dim}(\mathcal{N}_{i})=4
, by Center Manifold Theorem
[
19
]
, there exists a smooth manifold
\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
which is tangent to
\displaystyle\mathcal{N}
at
\displaystyle P^{*}_{i}
. Furthermore,
\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}
has dimension
\displaystyle 4
and is locally invariant under the flow of (
A.1
). We can write

V_{j}=\varphi_{j}(u,V_{i},\sigma),\quad j\neq i.

(A.7)

We further consider

\mathcal{D}_{i}:=\left\{\left|u-u^{*}\right|<\varepsilon,\,\left|V_{i}\right|<\varepsilon,\,\left|\sigma-\lambda_{i}(u^{*})\right|<\varepsilon\right\}.

(A.8)

Since
\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}
is tangent to
\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{i}
we have

\varphi_{j}(u,V_{i},\sigma)=\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\left|u-u^{*}\right|^{2}+\left|V_{i}\right|^{2}+\left|\sigma-\lambda_{i}(u^{*})\right|^{2}\right).

(A.9)

Note that equilibrium points
\displaystyle(u,0,\sigma)
with
\displaystyle\left|u-u^{*}\right|<\varepsilon,\left|\sigma-\lambda_{i}(u^{*})\right|<\varepsilon
lie in
\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}
then we have

\varphi_{j}(u,0,\sigma)=0\mbox{ for all }j\neq i.

(A.10)

Hence, we may write

\varphi_{j}(u,V_{i},\sigma)=\psi_{j}(u,V_{i},\sigma)V_{i},

(A.11)

for some
\displaystyle\psi_{j}
. Now, we would like to make a change of coordinates
\displaystyle V_{i}\mapsto\tilde{V}_{i}
as follows

\tilde{V}_{i}=l_{i}(u)\cdot v=V_{i}l_{i}(u)\cdot(r_{i}^{*}+\sum_{j\neq i}\psi_{j}(u,V_{i},\sigma)r_{j}^{*})

(A.12)

It implies that
\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{i}=\zeta_{ii}(u,V_{i},\sigma)V_{i}
with
\displaystyle\zeta_{ii}(u,V_{i},\sigma)=l_{i}(u)\cdot(r_{i}^{*}+\sum_{j\neq i}\psi_{j}(u,V_{i},\sigma)r_{j}^{*})

a
\displaystyle C^{2}
function such that
\displaystyle\zeta_{ii}(u^{*},0,\sigma)=1
. If we consider the function
\displaystyle f_{(u,\sigma)}(V_{i})=\zeta_{ii}(u,V_{i},\sigma)V_{i}
, we observe that
\displaystyle f_{(u,\sigma)}^{\prime}(V_{i})=\zeta_{ii}(u,V_{i},\sigma)+V_{i}\zeta_{ii,V_{i}}(u,V_{i},\sigma)\neq 0
in
\displaystyle{\cal D}_{i}
. It implies that
\displaystyle f_{(u,\sigma)}
is locally invertible and we can make the change of coordinates
\displaystyle V_{i}\mapsto\tilde{V}_{i}
.
Therefore, for any point
\displaystyle(u,v,\sigma)\in\mathcal{M}_{i}
we can write

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k}l_{k}(u)\cdot v\,r_{k}(u)=\sum\limits_{k}V_{i}l_{k}(u)\cdot(r_{i}^{*}+\sum_{j\neq i}\psi_{j}(u,V_{i},\sigma)r_{j}^{*})r_{k}(u)

(A.13)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k}l_{k}(u)\cdot v\,r_{k}(u)=\tilde{V}_{i}r_{i}(u)+\sum\limits_{k\neq i}\tilde{V}_{i}\frac{1}{\xi_{ii}(u,V_{i},\sigma)}l_{k}(u)\cdot(r_{i}^{*}+\sum_{j\neq i}\psi_{j}(u,V_{i},\sigma)r_{j}^{*})\,r_{k}(u)

\displaystyle=\tilde{V}_{i}\left(r_{i}(u)+\sum\limits_{j\neq i}\tilde{\psi}_{j}(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma)r_{j}(u)\right)=\tilde{V}_{i}\tilde{r_{i}}(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma),

with
\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{j}(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma)=\frac{1}{\xi_{ii}(u,V_{i},\sigma)}l_{j}(u)\cdot(r_{i}^{*}+\sum_{j\neq i}\psi_{j}(u,V_{i},\sigma)r_{j}^{*})
.
From (
A.9
) it follows that

\tilde{r}_{i}(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma)\rightarrow r_{i}^{*}\mbox{ as }(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma)\rightarrow(u^{*},0,\lambda_{i}(u^{*})).

(A.14)

Therefore, we may write
\displaystyle v=\tilde{V}_{i}\tilde{r}_{i}
and we have

\mathcal{M}_{i}=\left\{(u,v,\sigma_{i});\,\,v=\tilde{V}_{i}\tilde{r}_{i}(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma_{i})\right\}\mbox{ for }1\leq i\leq 2,

(A.15)

provided that
\displaystyle(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
are in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
\displaystyle(u^{*},0,\lambda_{i}(u^{*}))
. Next we derive few more estimates on
\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{k},\,k\neq i
.

Claim A.1
.

In a neighbourhood of
\displaystyle(u^{*},0,\lambda_{i}^{*})
we have

\tilde{\psi}_{k}(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma)=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\tilde{V}_{i}\right|.

(A.16)

Furthermore, we have

\tilde{\psi}_{k,\sigma}(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma),\tilde{\psi}_{k,u\sigma}(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma),\tilde{\psi}_{k,\sigma\sigma}(u,\tilde{V}_{i},\sigma)=\mathcal{O}(1)\left|\tilde{V}_{i}\right|.

(A.17)

Proof of Claim
A.1
:

Let us consider an
\displaystyle i
viscous travelling wave,
\displaystyle u(t,x)=U(x-\sigma t)
and assume that this solution is contained in the center manifold
\displaystyle{\cal M}_{i}
then we have
\displaystyle U^{\prime}(x)=\tilde{V}_{i}(x)\tilde{r}_{i}(U(x),\tilde{V}_{i}(x),\sigma)
and it yields

U^{\prime\prime}=\tilde{V}_{i}^{\prime}[\tilde{r}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i}\tilde{r}_{i,v}]+\tilde{V}^{2}_{i}\tilde{r}_{i,u}\tilde{r}_{i}.

(A.18)

Then we have

\tilde{V}_{i}^{\prime}B(u)[\tilde{r}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i}\tilde{r}_{i,v}]+\tilde{V}^{2}_{i}B\tilde{r}_{i,u}\tilde{r}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i}^{2}\tilde{r}_{i}\bullet DB(u)\tilde{r}_{i}=\tilde{V}_{i}(A(u)-\sigma)\tilde{r}_{i}.

(A.19)

We have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{i}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\langle B(u)[\tilde{r}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i}\tilde{r}_{i,v}],\tilde{r}_{i}\rangle}(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-\sigma\|\tilde{r}_{i}\|^{2})\tilde{V}_{i}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\tilde{V}_{i}\right|^{2}\right),

(A.20)

with
\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}_{i}=\langle A(u)\tilde{r}_{i}\,\tilde{r}_{i}\rangle
. It gives then

\frac{1}{\langle B(u)[\tilde{r}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i}\tilde{r}_{i,v}],\tilde{r}_{i}\rangle}(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-\sigma\|\tilde{r}_{i}\|^{2})\tilde{V}_{i}B(u)[\tilde{r}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i}\tilde{r}_{i,v}]+\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\tilde{V}_{i}\right|^{2}\right)=\tilde{V}_{i}(A(u)-\sigma)\tilde{r}_{i}.

(A.21)

Dividing by
\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{i}
and passing
\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{i}\rightarrow 0
, we obtain

\frac{1}{\langle B(u)\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma),\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)\rangle}(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)-\sigma\|\tilde{r_{i}}(u,0,\sigma)\|^{2})B(u)\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)=(A(u)-\sigma)\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma).

(A.22)

Set
\displaystyle b(u,0,\sigma)=\langle B(u)\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma),\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)\rangle>0
. We get

\displaystyle\displaystyle B(u)\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\left(Br_{i}(u)+\sum\limits_{j\neq i}\tilde{\psi}_{j}(u,0,\sigma)Br_{j}(u)\right)

(A.23)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\left(\alpha_{i}(u)r_{i}(u)+\sum\limits_{j\neq i}\tilde{\psi}_{j}(u,0,\sigma)\alpha_{j}(u)r_{j}(u)\right),

(A.24)

\displaystyle\displaystyle A(u)\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\left(Ar_{i}(u)+\sum\limits_{j\neq i}\tilde{\psi}_{j}(u,0,\sigma)Ar_{j}(u)\right)

(A.25)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\left(\lambda_{i}(u)r_{i}(u)+\sum\limits_{j\neq i}\tilde{\psi}_{j}(u,0,\sigma)\lambda_{j}(u)r_{j}(u)\right).

(A.26)

Applying above identities on (
A.22
) we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)-\sigma\|\tilde{r_{i}}(u,0,\sigma)\|^{2})}{b(u,0,\sigma)}\alpha_{i}(u)r_{i}(u)+\frac{(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)-\sigma\|\tilde{r_{i}}(u,0,\sigma)\|^{2})}{b(u,0,\sigma)}

(A.27)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\times\sum\limits_{j\neq i}\tilde{\psi}_{j}(u,0,\sigma)\alpha_{j}(u)r_{j}(u)=(\lambda_{i}(u)-\sigma)r_{i}(u)+\sum\limits_{j\neq i}\tilde{\psi}_{j}(u,0,\sigma)(\lambda_{j}(u)-\sigma)r_{j}(u).

(A.28)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)-\sigma\|\tilde{r_{i}}(u,0,\sigma)\|^{2})}{b(u,0,\sigma)}\alpha_{i}(u)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\lambda_{i}(u)-\sigma,

(A.29)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)-\sigma\|\tilde{r_{i}}(u,0,\sigma)\|^{2})}{b(u,0,\sigma)}\alpha_{j}(u)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\lambda_{j}(u)-\sigma\,\,\mbox{ if }\,\,\tilde{\psi}_{j}(u,0,\sigma)\neq 0.

(A.30)

This implies since
\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)=\langle A(u)\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)\,\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)\rangle
and using the fact that
\displaystyle\tilde{r}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)
and
\displaystyle\sigma
are respectively close from
\displaystyle r_{i}^{*}
and
\displaystyle\lambda_{i}^{*}
we can find
\displaystyle\varepsilon_{0}
small enough such that we have

c_{0}\leq\left|\lambda_{i}(u)-\lambda_{j}(u)\right|=\frac{\left|\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(u,0,\sigma)-\sigma\|\tilde{r_{i}}(u,0,\sigma)\|^{2}\right|}{b(u,0,\sigma)}\left|\alpha_{i}(u)-\alpha_{j}(u)\right|\leq\varepsilon_{0}.

(A.31)

This is a contradiction. Hence,
\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{j}(u,0,\sigma)=0
for all
\displaystyle j\neq i
.

∎
